the EIS relates;

▪ addresses the Planning Secretary’s environmental assessment

▪ identifies and addresses the relevant statutory requirements for

▪ has been prepared having regard to the Department’s State

as a whole, having regard to the economic, environmental and

sustainable development; 
▪ contains a consolidated description of the project in a single 
chapter of the EIS; 
▪ contains an accurate summary of the findings of any community 
engagement; and 
▪ contains an accurate summary of the detailed technical

P a g e  1  
SUMMARY 
This Environmental Impact Statement (EIS) has been prepared by Willowtree Planning Pty Ltd 
(Willowtree Planning), on behalf of ISPT Pty Ltd. The EIS is submitted to the New South Wales (NSW) 
Department of Planning , Housing and Infrastructure (DPHI), in support of an application for State 
Significant Development (SSD), for  the construction and operation of a data centre and associated 
infrastructure and amenities,  identified as the Julius Avenue Data Centre, comprising the following 
scope of works: 
 
• Site preparation works, including tree removal;  
• Earthworks and additional site retaining;   
• Infrastructure comprising civil works and utilities servicing;   
• Construction a data centre, with the following:  
o Basement car parking for 54 cars, including 2 accessible;  
o 12 data halls across six (6) storeys with an IT load of 115.2 Megawatts (MW) and a maximum 
power consumption of 169 MW, with upper -level mechanical equipment and rooftop 
plant;  
o Five (5) storey office/front of house building;  
o Six (6) storey enclosed generator gantry to rear of data centre.  
• Provision for a future Ausgrid precinct -wide 132 kilovolt (KV)  Sub-transmission Switching 
Station (STSS);  
• One (1) new street along the southern part of the Site;  
• Two (2) new pedestrian through-site links connecting Julius Avenue to the southern bushland; 
and 
• Complementary landscaping and offset planting; 
 
The Proposal is to be located at 6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde, legally described as Lot 89 in Deposited 
Plan 1082131. Such land is described throughout this EIS as the Site.  
 
The Site is located within the Ryde Local Government Area (LGA) and is zoned E3 Productivity Support, 
pursuant to the Ryde Local Environmental Plan 2014 (RLEP 2014). The Proposal is categorised as a data 
centre, which is permitted with consent in the E3 Productivity Support zone of the RLEP 2014. 
The Proposal is classified as SSD on the basis that it falls within the requirements of Section 25 of 
Schedule 1 of the of State Environmental Planning Policy (Planning Systems) 2021 (Planning Systems 
SEPP), being:  
 
25   Data centres 
(1) Development for the purpose of data centres that has a total power consumption of 
more than the relevant amount. 
(2) In this section— 
relevant amount means— 
(a) for development in relation to which the relevant environmental assessment 
requirements are notified under the Act on or before 31 May 2023 —10 megawatts, 
or

considered appropriate by the relevant legislation.

significant environmental impact. As such, it is recommended that the proposal be supported by the 
NSW DPHI for approval, subject to reasonable and relevant conditions. 
 
SITE CONTEXT 
 
The identified portion of land, that is the subject of this EIS is located at 6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde, 
legally described as Lot 89 in Deposited Plan 1082131 (the Site). 
 
The Site is irregular in shape and has a primary frontage of approximately 100m to Julius Avenue , a 
partial rear frontage to Richardson Place (cul -de-sac) at the north -eastern corner and has an area of 
approximately 2.863ha.    
  
The Site is currently vacant. The north-western part of the Site was excavated and cleared of vegetation 
in 2009 as part of Modified Determination No. 1395/1999 previously approved on the Site.  
 
The Site is zoned E3 Productivity Support pursuant to RLEP 2014 and is located within the City of Ryde 
LGA which is approximately 15km north-west of the Sydney CBD.

PROJECT DESCRIPTION  
 
The Proposal seeks consent for the construction and operation of a data centre and associated 
infrastructure and amenities, identified as the Julius Avenue Data Centre, comprising the following 
scope of works: 
 
• Site preparation works, including tree removal;  
• Earthworks and additional site retaining;   
• Infrastructure comprising civil works and utilities servicing;   
• Construction a data centre, with the following:  
o Basement car parking for 54 cars, including 2 accessible;  
o 12 data halls across six (6) storeys with an IT load of 115.2 MW and a maximum power 
consumption of 169 MW, with upper-level mechanical equipment and rooftop plant;  
o Five (5) storey office/front of house building;  
o Six (6) storey enclosed generator gantry to rear of data centre.  
• Provision for a future Ausgrid precinct-wide 132 KV STSS;  
• One (1) new street along the southern part of the Site;  
• Two (2) new pedestrian through-site links connecting Julius Avenue to the southern bushland; 
• Complementary landscaping and offset planting. 
 
PLANNING AND LEGISLATIVE FRAMEWORK  
 
All relevant Federal and State legislation, as well as Environmental Planning Instruments (EPIs), have 
been considered in the preparation of this EIS. The proposal is satisfactory in terms of its legislative 
context, on the basis that:  
 
▪ The Proposal is permissible in the zone;  
▪ The objectives of the zone are satisfied;  
▪ The range of applicable SEPPs have been considered;  
▪ Strategic documents that apply to the locality and wider region have identified that the 
proposed use is consistent with the strategic context of the area; and 
▪ The proposed development can satisfy the relevant provisions of the National Construction 
Code (NCC) and applicable Australian Standards. 
 
Refer to PART 4 of this EIS.

PLANNING AND ENVIRONMENTAL ASSESSMENT 
 
An assessment of environmental impact has been undertaken against the relevant planning controls 
and policies. Additionally, several expert consultants have been engaged to specifically consider 
relevant aspects of the Proposal. The EIS has found that  the Proposal generally complies with the 
relevant controls, and it is considered that appropriate mitigation measures can be put in place to 
minimise any identified risks. 
 
The RLEP 2014  is the principal EPI applying to the Site. The site is zoned E 3 Productivity Support  
pursuant to the RLEP 2014 . Under Clause 4.3 and Clause 4.4 of RLEP 2014 , the Site is subject to a 
maximum building height development standard of 30m and maximum FSR development standard 
of 1:1 . Notwithstanding, the Site benefits from an incentive maximum building height  of 45m  and 
incentive maximum FSR of 1.5:1 pursuant to Clause 7.7 of RLEP 2014. 
 
The Proposal has an FSR of 0.9 2:1 and therefore complies with the maximum FSR development 
standard permitted under Clause 4.4 of RLEP 2014, with the Site area calculated in accordance with 
Clause 4.5 of RLEP 2014  
 
The Proposal results in a maximum building height of 50.93m and therefore does not comply with the 
maximum incentive building height development standard prescribed by Clause 7.7 of RLEP 2014. 
 
A written request to vary this development standard utilising Clause 4.6 of RLEP 2014 accompanies this 
EIS at Appendix 34 and demonstrates that compliance with the incentive building height control is 
unreasonable and unnecessary in the circumstances, and that sufficient environmental planning 
grounds are available to justify the variation.  
 
The Proposal has been assessed against the relevant statutory requirements, and all preconditions and 
mandatory considerations have been assessed within this EIS and the supporting technical 
documentation. The matters identified in the SEARs issued for the Site have be en assessed in detail, 
with specialist reports underpinning the key findings and recommendations identified in the 
assessment provided in PART 6 of this EIS.  
 
[[page 19]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
 P a g e  6 
This EIS has been used to identify the potential environmental impacts and provide advice on 
appropriate mitigation. It has been demonstrated that the likely impacts identified in the assessment 
of the key issues will either be positive or can be appropriately mit igated through the undertakings 
made by the Proponent to manage and minimise potential impacts arising from the proposal. 
 
JUSTIFICATION FOR THE PROPOSED DEVELOPMENT  
 
Thorough consideration of the environmental impacts of the Proposal has been undertaken in the 
environmental impact assessment process and in the preparation of the EIS. In assessing the impacts 
of the Proposal, consideration has been given to social, economic and environmental matters. As 
identified in this EIS, Proposal is not considered to represent an environmental risk, or a development 
that might be out of context with the surrounding locality. 
 
EIS FINDINGS 
 
The findings of this EIS demonstrate that the Proposal can proceed with consent. All assessed impacts 
have been examined and deemed acceptable, in relation to all the relevant legislative requirements 
applicable to the Site. Furthermore, the proposed development aligns with the objectives of the A 
Metropolis of Three Cities – Greater Sydney Region Plan, the Northern District Plan and RLEP 2014.   
 
Based on the findings of this EIS, the Site can successfully support the proposed data centre, inclusive 
of related development and operations, with acceptable environmental impacts. The Proposal is a 
logical addition to the existing Macquarie Park Precinct and is consistent with the zone objectives and 
strategic policies for the Site.    
 
The proposed development is deemed suitable for its intended purpose, having regard to its regional 
and local context and would not result in any significant environmental impacts, subject to appropriate 
mitigation measures. As such, it is requested that the Proposal be approved, subject to reasonable and 
relevant conditions. 
 
Given the above reasons and the satisfaction of both of the Objects of the EP&A Act and the aims of the 
RLEP 2014 , it is recommended that the Proposal be supported subject to relevant and reasonable 
conditions.

PART 1 INTRODUCTION 
1.1 INTRODUCTION 
This Environmental Impact Statement (EIS) has been prepared by Willowtree Planning Pty Ltd 
(Willowtree Planning), on behalf of ISPT Pty Ltd (the Proponent). The EIS is submitted to the New South 
Wales (NSW) Department of Planning, Housing and Infrastructure (DPHI), in support of an application 
for State Significant Development (SSD), for the construction and operation of a data centre and 
associated infrastructure and amenities, identified as the Julius Avenue Data Centre, comprising the 
following scope of works: 
 
• Site preparation works, including tree removal;  
• Earthworks and additional site retaining;   
• Infrastructure comprising civil works and utilities servicing;   
• Construction a data centre, with the following:  
o Basement car parking for 54 cars, including 2 accessible;  
o 12 data halls across six (6) storeys with an IT load of 115.2 MW and a maximum power 
consumption of 169 MW, with upper-level mechanical equipment and rooftop plant;  
o Five (5) storey office/front of house building;  
o Six (6) storey enclosed generator gantry to rear of data centre.  
• Provision for a future Ausgrid precinct-wide 132 KV STSS;  
• One (1) new street along the southern part of the Site;  
• Two (2) new pedestrian through-site links connecting Julius Avenue to the southern bushland; 
and 
• Complementary landscaping and offset planting; 
 
The Proposal is to be located at 6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde, legally described as Lot 89 in Deposited 
Plan 1082131. Such land is described throughout this EIS as the Site.  
 
The Site is located within the City of Ryde LGA and is zoned E3 Productivity Support, pursuant to the 
RLEP 2014. The Proposal  is categorised as a data centre, which is permitted with consent in the E3 
Productivity Support zone of the RLEP 2014. 
The Proposal is classified as SSD on the basis that it falls within the requirements of Section 25 of 
Schedule 1 of the of State Environmental Planning Policy (Planning Systems) 2021 (Planning Systems 
SEPP). 
 
This EIS describes the Site and proposed development. It also responds to the SEARs and assesses the 
proposed development in terms of all relevant matters set out in legislation, EPIs and associated 
planning policies.

Development Guidelines – Preparing an Environmental Impact Statement as follows:

1.6 EMPLOYMENT NUMBERS 
The following employment numbers are estimated for the proposal. 
 
1.6.1 Construction Jobs 
 
An estimate of labour work force for the Proposal is calculated based on the following parameters: 
 
▪ Direct employment on site – 3,424.14 
▪ Manufacture and supply of intermediate goods and services – 1,017.10 
▪ Indirect supply of goods and services – 522.24 
 
Based on the above, the Proposal is estimated to generate 4,963.49 full-time equivalent jobs 
consistently throughout the lifetime of the development build.   
 
1.6.2 Operational Jobs 
 
An estimate of operational employments numbers has been calculated based on the operative 
requirements of the future tenant and the number of shifts per day.  
 
Based on the parameters provided by the Proponent, the total number of operational jobs for the 
Proposal is 50.

Surrounding land uses 
 
Existing attributes of the Site and surrounding context are noted as follows: 
 
▪ The Site is generally surrounded to the north -east, north-west, and south-west by commercial 
uses and E3 Productivity Support zoned land.  
▪ Approximately 150m to the west of the Site is MU1 mixed Use zoned land which contemplates 
residential development to a height of 95m.  
▪ The Site directly adjoins the Lane Cove National Park to the east and south-east which contains 
a walking track known as The Great North Walk and a fire trail.  
▪ The Lane Cove River is located approximately 100m to the east of the Site.  
▪ The Site directly adjoins the Ryde City Council Administrative Centre to the east.  
▪ Approximately 500m to the west of the Site is the North Ryde Metro Station.  
▪ The nearest residential development is located approximately 300m to the south -east of the 
Site on the eastern side of Lane Cove River in Lane Cove North, and 300m to the south -west of 
the Site on the southern side of Epping Road in North Ryde.

Land surrounding the Site comprises the following land use zones pursuant to RLEP 2014: 
  
▪ E3 Productivity Support to the north-east, north-west and south-west.

▪ RE1 Public Recreation on the eastern side of the Lane Cove River

Park), located  directly to the east  of the Site, containing Bushfire Prone Land, Riparian Lands and 
Watercourses and Coastal Wetlands and Proximity Area for Coastal Wetlands. 
 
The closest residential suburbs are approximately 350m to the south-west (North Ryde) and 600m and 
300m east (Lane Cove North) of the Site.

6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde (89/DP1082131)

Current use The Site is currently vacant.    
Topography  Ground surface levels within the Site typically fall steeply from the street 
level (approximately 48 m AHD) to the previously excavated opencut 
sandstone pad which covers the northern portion of the Site (at 
approximately 35 m AHD).  
The land in the southern portion of the Site then falls steeply to the south 
towards Lane Cove River, with the southern boundary being at 
approximately 24 m AHD as shown in Figure 5 below.  
Access The current vehicular access to the Site is via Richardson Place to the 
south-east and via the shared driveway along the western boundary from 
Julius Avenue.   
Vegetation  The northern portion of the Site has been heavily disturbed due to 
excavation of sandstone bedrock in 2009 and contains only regrowth 
vegetation. The southern portion of the Site contains mostly native forest 
vegetation in moderate to good condition. The remaining parts of the Site 
contain hardstands in the form of exposed sandstone bedrock and 
outcrops, and non-native vegetation containing exotic thickets 
dominated by privet and lantana. 
Watercourses  There are no watercourses located on the Site.  
Wetlands  No mapped local or important wetlands occur within the Site.   
Biodiversity  No areas of outstanding biodiversity value lie within the Site.  
Easements and 
encumbrances 
The Site is burdened by several easements and encumbrances, pertaining 
to various infrastructure services, including water, sewerage and 
electricity, and access. Further details are included on the Title 
Documents provided at Appendix 4. 
Heritage  The Site has been the subject of an Aboriginal Cultural Heritage 
Assessment Report (ACHAR), and Heritage Due Diligence Assessment (see 
Appendix 26 and Appendix 27 respectively). The Site has been assessed as 
unlikely to contain any intact Aboriginal sites and has therefore been 
assessed as holding low archaeological potential.  
Furthermore, the Site is not listed on the State Heritage Register (or subject 
to an interim heritage order) under the NSW Heritage Act 1977 and is not 
identified as an item of environmental heritage  or within a heritage 
conservation area on any EPIs.

2.7 STRATEGIC PLANNING CONTEXT 
2.7.1 Greater Sydney Region Plan – A Metropolis of Three Cities 
 
The Greater Sydney Region Plan – A Metropolis of Three Cities divides the Sydney Region into three (3) 
Cities, with a vision of growth until 2056. The Plan aims to anticipate the housing and employment 
needs of a growing and vastly changing population. The overall vision pursues an objective of 
transforming ‘Greater Sydney’ into a Metropolis of Three Cities, including:  
  
▪ The Western Parkland City;  
▪ The Central River City; and   
▪ The Eastern Harbour City. 
 
The division into three cities puts workers and the wider community closer to an array of characteristics 
such as, intensive jobs, ‘city -scale’ infrastructure and services, entertainment and cultural facilities. By 
managing and retaining industrial land cl ose to city centres and transport, this will ensure critical and 
essential services are readily available to support local businesses and community members and 
residents. Once constructed and operational, the Site would achieve economic growth and prosperity, 
as well as encourage employment-generating opportunities within an area zoned for such permissible 
purposes, that is well connected to public transport, providing an ease of commute. 
 
The Site and Proposal contribute to the objectives set out in the Greater Sydney Region Plan  – A 
Metropolis of Three Cities by promotion of technological advancements and employment -generating 
opportunities to the wider locality and community, positioned within the Macquarie Park Precinct.    
 
The Proposal is consistent with the 10 Directions and planning priorities described in the Region Plan, 
specifically in the following manner: 
 
▪ Infrastructure and collaboration – once in operation, the development would be able to provide 
secure, reliable and adaptable solutions for cloud, content and large enterprise customers to 
house their high and rapidly growing volumes of data and information. The infrastructure has 
been designed to meet future needs. 
▪ Liveability – the Proposal encourages employment -generating opportunities and economic 
prosperity, which has positive influences on the wider locality.  
▪ Productivity – the Proposal is situated within the North District Plan  and will support the 
retention and efficient use of industrial land; and,   
▪ Sustainability – the Proposal would not exhibit or emit any detrimental impacts to its wider 
ecological surroundings and is consistent with the principles of ESD.   
 
The Site is located within the Eastern Harbour City . The Proposal aligns with the vision and objectives 
for the Eastern Economic Corridor in that the Proposal will facilitate the provision of employment and 
economic activity which is well connected to transport links. The Eastern Economic Corridor extends 
from Macquarie Park to Sydney Airport and is an important contributor to the economic growth of the 
state.  
 
2.7.2 North District Plan 
 
The Site forms part of the North District, as identified in Figure 9 below and is subject to the provisions 
of the North District Plan.

North District Plan  establishes a number of priorities and actions to guide growth, development and

The Greater Sydney Commission’s mission statement further reinforces the Plan’s concentrated aims 
by outlining its main strategies, namely:  
 
▪ Enhancing the role of the Eastern Economic Corridor, including North Sydney as part of the 
Harbour CBD; 
▪ Supporting jobs growth in strategic centres, including health and education precincts and

2.7.3 Planning Ryde Local Strategic Planning Statement 2020 
 
The Planning Ryde Local Strategic Planning Statement 2020 (Ryde LSPS) applies to the Site. The Ryde 
LSPS outlines the 20-year planning vision for the City of Ryde LGA. The plan aims to create a liveable, 
prosperous and connected city that  that provides for the LGA’s future needs while protecting nature 
and history.  
 
The Proposal is aligned within the following  Planning Priorities for the City of Ryde LGA, as outlined in 
the Ryde LSPS: 
 
▪ Planning Priority D2: Ensure all new buildings throughout the City of Ryde are examples of 
excellent design. 
▪ Planning Priority IN1: Align growth with infrastructure: provide sufficient infrastructure to 
support current and future population growth 
▪ Planning Priority M1: Develop Macquarie Park as a health and education strategic centre 
focused around activity hubs, including Macquarie Park Station. 
▪ Planning Priority M2 Ensure the economic vitality of the Macquarie Park Health and Education 
Strategic Centre and retain the precinct’s strong economic base as shown by its contribution 
to Australia’s GDP 
▪ Planning Priority M5: Create a public domain within Macquarie Park that brings people 
together 
▪ Planning Priority M6: Continue to develop Macquarie Park into a sustainable centre 
▪ Planning Priority M7: Promote and encourage a more walkable environment within Macquarie 
Park 
▪ Planning Priority OS1: Provide accessible open space to service population needs 
▪ Planning Priority OS2: Ensure open space can be shared and enjoyed by all 
▪ Planning Priority OS5: Sustain open space for existing and future populations 
▪ Planning Priority E1: Protect and enhance bushland, biodiversity, environmentally sensitive 
waterways, scenic and cultural landscapes 
 
The Site is located within the Macquarie Park Precinct land as identified in the LSPS, which is home to 
96.500 jobs, and contributes $15.47 billion to GRP.  
 
Planning Priorities M1 and M2 of the Ryde LSPS seek to ensure the economic vitality of the Macquarie 
Park Health and Education Strategic Centre and retain the precinct’s strong economic base as shown 
by its contribution to Australia’s GDP . The Proposal represents a significant investment in the Precinct 
and will offer crucial data storage capacity near key health and education institutions, supporting the 
infrastructure needs of these sectors.  
 
Planning Priority M6 and E1  seek to protect and enhance bushland, biodiversity, environmentally 
sensitive waterways, scenic and cultural landscapes. The Proposal involves significant enhancement to 
the bushland on the Site and protects important biodiversity. 
 
The Proposal is also consistent with Planning Priorities M5, M6, M7, OS1, OS2, and OS5 by providing 
recreation space that can be shared and enjoyed by all.

2.7.4 Better Placed 
 
This policy establishes a baseline of what is expected to achieve good design, across all projects in NSW. 
Good design is a phrase that encapsulates the aspirations of Better Placed including its vision for NSW, 
its definition of good process, and its outline of objectives for the built environment.  
  
Better Placed defines seven (7) objectives for good design. The Proposal is considered to align with the 
Better Placed framework, as documented in the Architectural Design Report and Landscape Design 
Report provided to support this EIS.  
 
2.7.5 Future Transport Strategy 2056 
 
The Future Transport Strategy 2056 is a 40-year strategy, supported by plans for regional NSW and for 
Greater Sydney. The strategy and plans focus on the role of transport in delivering movement and place 
outcomes that support the character of the places and communities that are desired for the future. 
 
The Proposal aligns with the strategies of the Future Transport Strategy 2056 on the following basis: 
 
▪ the Site has access to regular public transport services and is accessible by active transport; 
▪ parking provision is appropriate;  
▪ access, servicing and internal layout will be provided in accordance with Australian Standards 
AS2890.1-2004 and AS2890.2-2018; and 
▪ the surrounding road network and intersections will be able to cater for the proposed 
development traffic. 
2.8 SITE SUITABILITY 
The Site is located within the Macquarie Park Precinct and is zoned E3 Productivity Support pursuant 
to RLEP 2014. The Proposal would facilitate the  intended use of the Site for employment purposes, 
which is consistent with the zoning and the desired future context.  
 
The Proposal falls within the definition of a light industry which encompasses high technology industry 
and furthermore a data centre, which is defined as follows: 
 
Data centre means a building or place the principal purpose of which is to collect, distribute, 
process or store electronic data using information technology,  
 
Data centres are therefore permitted  with consent in the E3 Productivity Support pursuant to RLEP 
2014. The Site is suitable for the size and scale of the development proposed and represents a quality 
outcome to facilitate a high -technology industrial use that is commensurate to the surrounding area 
and its existing built form.  Consistency with the objectives of the zone is discussed in Appendix B of 
this EIS.  
 
In summary, the Site is highly suited to accommodate the Proposal based on the following factors:  
 
▪ RLEP 2014 allows for the Proposal as a permissible use; 
▪ The Site is readily accessible via the regional road network and metro rail, and does not generate 
significant traffic volumes; 
▪ The Proposal is compatible with surrounding development and local context; 
▪ The Site can be serviced immediately and at no cost to Government; 
▪ The Proposal causes minimal impact on the environment , with the development f ootprint 
focussed on already cleared land;  
▪ The proposed built form is designed to mitigate any impacts on surrounding properties; and  
▪ The Proposal is consistent with strategic intent of the area.  
[[page 39]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
 P a g e  26 
The following key elements of the Site and proposed development are noted:  
 
2.8.1 Visual Impact 
 
The Visual Impact Assessment prepared for the Site confirms that the Proposal will create minor to 
high visual impacts from the twenty -three (23) viewpoints assessed. The assessment confirms the 
moderate to high/moderate visual impacts are generally limited to those viewpoints within proximity 
to the Site, apart from VP3 which is approximately 500m to the south -east, however, has a direct 
sightline toward the proposed development. From longer distance views, such as those experienced in 
residential areas of North Ryde and Lane Cove North, the Proposal would only result in a small change 
in view when compared to a visual baseline. The VIA concludes that despite these impacts, the Proposal 
is considered to be acceptable give n the general compliance with built form controls applying to the 
Site and the desired future character of the area.  
 
2.8.2 Noise and Vibration 
 
Construction and operational noise impacts from the proposed data centre have been assessed, 
potential noise impacts identified, and a range of feasible and reasonable mitigation measures 
incorporated into the Proposal design to minimise noise impacts on s ensitive receivers nearby and 
further removed from the Site. Subject to the appropriate mitigation measures, the Site is considered 
suitable for the Proposal in relation to noise and vibration.  
 
2.8.3 Air Quality 
 
Based on the findings of the Air Quality Impact Assessment carried out for the Proposal, the predicted 
incremental concentrations for the worst -case operating scenario of the development shows that no 
exceedances are predicted to occur at all surrounding receptors for all pollutants assessed in the AQIA. 
Correspondingly, it is anticipated that under operation of the generator testing schedule that no 
significant air quality impacts are predicted to be experienced at sensitive receptors. Therefore, the Site 
is suitable having consideration to potential air quality impacts of the Proposal.   
 
2.8.4 Transport and Traffic 
 
The Traffic and Accessibility Impact Assessment carried out for the Proposal confirms that  traffic and 
parking implications are not expected  to result in any discernible adverse impact on the surrounding 
road network, with management measures to ensure minimal traffic and parking implications for both 
the construction and operation of the Proposal. Refer to the traffic and parking assessment provided at 
Section 6.1.6 of this EIS which demonstrates that the Proposal is suitable for the Site on traffic and 
parking grounds.  
 
2.8.5 Infrastructure 
 
The Site is suitably located with access to infrastructure and utility services – refer to Section 6.1.21 of 
this EIS. 
 
2.8.6 Heritage 
 
The Aboriginal Cultural Heritage Assessment Report carried out for the Site concludes that no 
Aboriginal sites or objects have been identified within the Site. The Proposal will not impact on the 
heritage significance of any identified heritage items or heritage conservation areas, and the Site is of 
low archaeological sensitivity.  
 
  
[[page 40]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
 P a g e  27 
2.8.7 Stormwater and Flooding 
 
The increase in local post -development flows from the site will be managed prior to discharge to 
downstream waterways and will not adversely affect any land, drainage system or watercourse as a 
result of the development. The proposed stormwater quantity an d quality strategy is discussed in 
Section 6.1.13 of this EIS. 
 
The Flood Impact and Risk Assessment concludes  the Proposal is demonstrated to have no adverse 
flood impacts on adjacent or downstream properties and t he Site can be developed and operated 
safely, with appropriate design measures to manage residual flood risk, as discussed in Section 6.1.14 of 
this EIS. 
 
2.8.8 Summary of Site Suitability  
 
The Site’s consistency with applicable regional and local strategies is demonstrated in the 
comprehensive environmental assessment, provided in PART 6 of this EIS, which includes an analysis 
of all potential impacts, which has been informed by the relevant consultant reports. Accordingly, the 
environmental assessment prescribes recommendations and mitigation measures (where necessary), 
to account for all identified potential impacts, by the proposed development.  
 
Having regard to the characteristics of the Site and its location, the Proposal is suitable for the Site for 
the reasons below:   
 
▪ The site is zoned E3 Productivity Support which permits development for the purpose of data 
centres with consent. The Proposal is consistent with the objectives for the E3 zone.  
▪ The Site remains capable of being appropriately serviced to accommodate the proposed 
development.  
▪ The Site is well located in close proximity to arterial and major collector roads and will not have 
any adverse traffic impact on the area due to the low-traffic generation of the Proposal. 
▪ The Site is well-serviced by a range of public transport options that provide a direct connection 
to the CBD and surrounding suburbs. Access to suitable transportation options is an essential 
requirement for employment-generating uses.  
▪ The Site benefits from geographical separation from sensitive receivers and is suitably located 
within an established employment precinct.  
▪ The Site can appropriately accommodate the proposed development while balancing 
environmental and design consideration and preserving the amenity of neighbouring 
properties. 
▪ The architectural design will deliver a high quality and modern data centre development, 
▪ The Site will provide important infrastructure to service the local and regional area. 
 
The technical assessments prepared in support of the Proposal have concluded that the Site is suitable 
for the proposed development. 
 
PART 2 of this EIS demonstrates the Site’s suitability for the Proposal.

PART 3 PROJECT DESCRIPTION  
3.1 OBJECTIVES OF THE PROPOSAL 
The aim of the Proposal  is to provide a purpose -built development encompassing a data centre and 
associated infrastructure and amenities in line with Industry Best Practice, resulting in: 
 
• Support the growth and transformation of IT sector;  
• Generate employment – during construction and once the development is operational; 
• Improve access to jobs for residents of the immediate community and wider locality; 
• Supplement, support and complement the Macquarie Park Precinct and its strategic intent; 
• Demonstrate architectural excellence, through siting and design compatibility, with minimal 
visual impact; and 
• Provide suitable mitigation measures where required, to minimise any unforeseen impacts 
arising in the future. 
3.2 PROJECT OVERVIEW 
Consent is sought for the construction and operation of a data centre and associated infrastructure and 
amenities, in accordance with the following particulars:

3.3 DEVELOPMENT DESCRIPTION 
3.3.1 Site Preparation 
 
3.3.1.1 Earthworks 
 
Earthworks are proposed as part of the development to accommodate a building pad. The extent of 
excavation is not significant given the existing excavation previously carried out on  the Site as shown 
on the Bulk Earthworks Cut/Fill Plan prepared for the Proposal (see Appendix 21). The extent of bulk 
earthworks is provided below: 
 
▪ Total cut volume: 69,292.58m3 
▪ Total fill volume: 1,669.09m3 
▪ Balance: Cut 67,623.49m3 
 
An Erosion and Sediment Control Plan is provided at Appendix A to the Water  Management Plan at 
Appendix 21  of this EIS. Erosion and Sediment Control measures  will be implemented during 
earthworks to manage the impacts of erosion and sedimentation.   
 
3.3.1.2 Demolition  
 
The Proposal does not involve any demolition works. The Site was previously cleared and excavated as 
part of Determination No. 1395/1999. No structures currently exist on the Site for demolition.  
 
3.3.1.3 Tree Removal 
 
A total of 509 individual trees (in 188 tree groups) require removal as they are located within the 
proposed works footprint or are significantly impacted upon by the works. A total of 38 trees are  
proposed to be  retained and protected throughout the construction works , not including trees to be 
retained within the dense bushland area which is not proposed to be disturbed. A total of 143 new trees 
are proposed as part of the landscaping design.  
 
The trees to be removed and those to be retained are detailed in the Arboricultural Impact Assessment 
provided as an appendix to the Landscape Plan at Appendix 12. The extent of tree removal is further 
outlined in Section 6.1.7.1 of this EIS. All tree removal is to be undertaken by a suitably qualified arborist 
(minimum AQF level 3).  
 
3.3.2 Site Servicing and Utilities 
 
An assessment has been undertaken to determine the capacity for the Site to be serviced by relevant 
infrastructure and utilities. The Infrastructure Delivery, Management and Staging Plan pr ovided at  
Appendix 29 identifies the existing services and infrastructure within the vicinity of the Site and the 
proposed augmentation connection required to service the Proposal. 
 
3.3.2.1 Ausgrid Sub-Transmission Switching Station 
 
The Proposal includes provision for a new Ausgrid precinct -wide STSS to service the Site. The STSS is 
not proposed to be delivered as part of the Proposal and will form  part of a separate approval. 
Notwithstanding, the impacts of the STSS have been assessed as part of this EIS and p rovision for the 
STSS includes tree removal, civil works and building pad, and access and servicing to accommodate the 
STSS.  
 
  
[[page 44]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
 P a g e  31 
3.3.2.2 Electricity 
 
The new on -Site STSS of 132kV/33kV at N redundant arrangement with capacity of 180MVA will be 
constructed by Ausgrid. The STSS is sufficient to meet the electrical demand for the Proposal.  
 
Site 33kV MV cable reticulation from the new 132kV/33kV substation to the data centre building will be 
by means of underground cabling conduits and internal risers up to the transformer rooms. 
 
3.3.2.3 Stormwater  
 
The Proposal’s stormwater design will be serviced by a series of drainage elements which will discharge 
the Site’s catchment, with a total of two (2) OSD tanks on Site.  
 
The main building concrete roof is raked to allow capture of all rainwater via guttering to the north and 
south elevation. The generator structure concrete roof is raked to allow capture of all rainwater via 
guttering to the north elevation. All water will  be captured via downpipes routed internally to an On-
Site Detention (OSD) tank in the basement via a proposed rainwater tank. If the rainwater tank is already 
at capacity, the runoff will discharge directly into the OSD.  
 
The external pedestrian and vehicular pavement catchments will be accumulated via surface inlet pits 
or kerb inlet pits and routed to one of the two (2) OSD tanks on site.  
 
Both OSD tanks will contain water quality treatment units and will discharge into the lower -level 
bushland via the utilisation of level spreaders. The overland discharge to lower -level bushland was the 
preferred proposed discharge methodology as suggested by Council and agreed with NSW Parks and 
Wildlife Service in initial consultation sessions. 
 
3.3.2.4 Telecommunications 
 
New works for a telecommunications pit and duct will be required but will be delivered in a single stage 
to reflect the construction of the Proposal. The cable reticulation pathways will be co -ordinated with 
the various telecommunications carriers. 
 
3.3.2.5 Gas 
 
Connection to gas supply is not required for the Proposal. All power will be supplied via electrical mains 
and/or diesel generators. The existing gas network shall be protected throughout construction phase to 
the satisfaction of Jemena. 
 
3.3.2.6 Water 
 
New works for potable water will be required but will be delivered in a single stage to reflect the 
construction of the Proposal. The tapping points and metering will be co-ordinated with Sydney Water 
through their TAP-IN applications portal as required. The industrial water tanks and associated pumps 
within the Site will be utilised as required as the data centre’s mechanical demands grow over the 
operational lifecycle of the building.  Data halls will be progressively fitted out and plant will come 
online, and water use increased as the load from each data hall is progressively completed. 
 
3.3.2.7 Sewerage 
 
New works for sewer drainage will be required and will be delivered in a single stage to reflect the 
construction of the Proposal.  
 
  
[[page 45]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
 P a g e  32 
3.3.3 Physical Layout and Design  
 
3.3.3.1 Built Form 
 
Construction of the Proposal involves a new \data centre building, and associated access, fencing and 
landscaping. The building comprises the following: 
 
Basement Level 
 
• OSD, industrial water tanks, and fuel tanks 
• Carpark containing 54 car parking spaces, including 2 accessible spaces 
• 6 bicycle spaces

3.3.3.2 Building Design 
 
The proposed massing has been carefully designed to meet operational requirements while addressing 
key Site constraints, including setbacks, pedestrian pathways, and the preservation of existing 
vegetation. The building takes advantage of the Site’s unique topography. 
 
Positioned primarily in the northern portion of the Site, the building utilises an existing Site cut to 
reduce its overall height and visual bulk, minimising its impact on surrounding areas, particularly when 
viewed from Julius Avenue. This strategic placement ensures that the building maintains a low profile 
while maximising the use of available space. 
 
The main entry and Front of House (FOH) component are oriented to the north to optimise solar access 
for office and administration areas. Service and heavy vehicle access are discreetly located to the south, 
creating a clear separation between operational and visitor functions. Critical power ge nerators are 
positioned to the south of the building to minimise noise impact on nearby commercial and future 
residential areas to the north. This approach ensures that the building’s massing respects the 
surrounding environment and minimises potential disturbances, ensuring a balanced relationship 
between functionality, environmental impact, and integration with the surrounding context. 
 
The Proposal provides significant floor-to-floor clearances of 6m on all levels. An additional 9m rooftop 
plant and ventilation towers are required with acoustic screening, which is required to be located on 
the roof for operational reasons. The floor-to-floor heights required for a date centre development are 
precise and based on a required volumetric outcome to establish the future internal layouts whilst 
remaining compliant with BCA Standards and fire engineering requirements, e.g. ceiling clearances and 
fire sprinkler systems.  
 
The specific volumetric outcomes required for a data centre are guided by the below requirements: 
 
• Whitespace needing to accommodate bulk power and fibre cabling to meet the current cloud 
computing and AI demands; 
• Minimum services and computer server clearance required below ceiling level within the 
whitespace to enable safe access and maintenance works; and 
• High ceiling void needed to act as return air plenum which is essential to maintain efficient 
cooling to critical digital infrastructure. 
 
  
[[page 47]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
 P a g e  34 
3.3.3.3 Materials 
 
The service areas, particularly the stair cores, will be constructed from precast concrete with a rugged 
vertical finish. These robust elements ground the building and act as key structural anchors within the 
façade. In contrast, the FOH façade features a lighter -toned polished concrete that complements its 
role as the public-facing focal point.  
 
For the data halls, the façade will be clad in polished metal panels. This finish visually breaks up the 
large surface area without the use of windows —bringing depth and animation to the otherwise solid 
façade.

3.3.3.4 Building Footprint 
 
The Proposal has a gross floor area (GFA) of 26,076sqm on the 28,630sqm Site. Therefore, the Proposal 
has an FSR which equates to 0.91:1.  
 
It is noted that a base maximum FSR development standard of 1:1 applies to the Site pursuant to Clause 
4.4 of RLEP and an incentive maximum FSR development standard of 1.5:1 applies to the Site pursuant 
to Clause 7.7 of RLEP 2014. The Proposal does not rely on the incentive provisions and complies with the 
base maximum FSR development standard.  
 
The STSS has been excluded from the calculation of GFA pursuant to RLEP 2014 given that it does not 
form part of the Proposal, and consent is not sought for any works that would constitute GFA.  
 
3.3.3.5 Building Height 
 
The Proposal includes three (3) separate building forms of variable heights, as follows: 
 
• The main data hall building has a height of seven (7) storeys with rooftop plant 
• The office/FOH building has a height of five (5) storeys and wraps around the south -western 
corner of the Site 
• The enclosed generator gantry has a height of six (6) storeys to the rear of the data hall building.  
 
The office/FOH building presents a maximum building height of 38.93m to the Julius Avenue frontage, 
with the taller data hall portion of the building behind and above . Given the slope of the Site  and 
existing excavation carried out on the Site , the base of the building is substantially below the Julius 
Avenue footpath level , which significantly reduces the scale of the building when viewed from the 
public domain. The maximum height of the main data hall building in the centre of the Site is 50.93m, 
which constitutes the mesh screening to the rooftop plant in the north -eastern corner of the building. 
Notwithstanding, no part of the  substantive building breaches the building height standard , only 
rooftop plant screening. The enclosed generator gantry complies with the building height standard.  
 
The Site is subject to a 30m base maximum building height development standard pursuant to Clause 
4.3 of RLEP 2014 and an incentive maximum building height development standard of 45m pursuant 
to Clause 7.7 of RLEP 2014.  
 
The Proposal seeks a maximum building height of 50.93m, which exceeds the 45m incentive maximum 
building height development standard by 5.93m or 13.1%. The Proposal entails the following key 
building height components identified in TABLE 9 below. 
 
TABLE 9: BUILDING HEIGHT COMPONENTS 
Project element Proposed 
Incentive maximum building height development standard 45m 
Proposed maximum building height to Julius Avenue 38.93m 
Proposed maximum building height to top of building roof 42m 
Proposed maximum building height to top of rooftop plant 50.93m 
 
Notwithstanding the variation, compliance with the development standard is considered to be 
unreasonable and unnecessary given the circumstances, and there are sufficient environmental

3.3.4 Fencing 
 
The Proposal includes a variety of fencing types based on the security requirements of the Site and 
interface with the public domain.  
 
The primary security measure for the site is a 2400mm high spear top palisade fence. This robust 
fencing extends around the entire perimeter of the Site, with the exception of the front boundary to 
Julius Avenue, ensuring comprehensive security coverage.  
 
In contrast, the fencing along the north -western portion of the Site, which faces Julius Avenue, is 
1500mm high and constructed from a simple wire mesh. This design choice was influenced by the 
Council’s preference to maintain the commercial streetscape al ong Julius Avenue, avoiding the visual 
obstruction that more substantial fencing might cause. The wire mesh fencing was selected as an 
appropriate response to this concern, offering a high degree of transparency and minimal visual impact 
while still providing a secure delineation of the site.

The Proposal includes the provision of a new street through the Site connecting from Richardson Place

The street has a reserve of 10m allowing for dual carriageway and pedestrian footpaths along each side 
of the street.

The Proposal includes two (2) through -site links in accordance with the Macquarie Park Design Guide . 
The northern link runs from Julius Avenue to Richardson Place along the north -eastern Site boundary. 
The southern link runs from Julius Avenue adjacent to the driveway along the south -western Site 
boundary and connects with the footpath provided to the new street that dissects the Site.

The Proposal includes a new link between Richardson Place and the Great North Walk in Lane Cove 
National Park along the south-eastern portion of the Site through various walking tracks and lookouts. 
The location of these walking tracks is illustrated on the Landscape Plans.

3.3.9 Operational activities  
 
3.3.9.1 Use and Activities  
 
Recent times have shaped the way we live and work, resulting in an increasing demand for cloud-based 
services. Such demand is playing a key role in overall market growth of data centres. The Proposal seeks 
to design, construct and commission a data centre and associated infrastructure and amenities which 
would be brand new, multi -storey, world class, highly reliable facilities suitable for the needs of retail, 
wholesale and hyperscale computing customers.  
 
The Proposal is expected to provide ongoing reliable and resilient service to the highest standards 
expected of data centre operators and customers and would see the introduction of a new data centre 
facility for the greater Sydney area. The new development would be managed by a facilities 
management and security operations teams, operating under a centralised model, appropriately sized 
for this development.  
 
From a high level, the operational process on the Site is as follows:  
 
▪ Data halls are provided with uninterruptable power and cooling to maintain the operating 
conditions for customer IT equipment. The facilities management team would monitor, 
manage, and maintain the power and cooling systems to ensure high uptime consistent  with 
data centre requirements.  
▪ On-site security personnel provide monitoring, patrols, event response and related security 
operations function. 
▪ Waste generated predominantly comprises packaging materials for new equipment, and old 
electronic components. These materials are transported internally through the facility to the 
waste collection areas adjacent to loading bays for collection by contracted recycling and waste 
services. E-waste would be stored and collected separately.  
 
3.3.9.2 Office & Tech areas 
 
The Proposal includes offices for staff and breakout spaces throughout the building which cater to 
customer requirements which may include storage or additional staff working and meeting spaces.  
 
3.3.9.3 Hours of operation 
 
The facility is proposed to operate 24 hours a day, seven days a week. Noting the main business activities 
including deliveries, maintenance and customer equipment connections would typically be conducted 
during business hours Monday to Saturday. 
 
3.3.9.4 Staff 
 
The Proposal has been designed to accommodate 50 staff at maximum capacity.  This figure matches 
the staffing levels of other data centre projects of similar scale. Anticipated staffing numbers have been 
calculated in the EDC Report.

3.3.10 Plant and Equipment 
 
3.3.10.1 Back-up Power System 
 
The Site is designed for a total of 72 low voltage 3 MW generators for the IT load within the Data Halls 
and one (1) low voltage 1MW generator for the office /FOH and associated safety services. These 
generators will only operate in the event of a mains utility failure to the respective electrical block or for 
testing purposes. These generators support a total load capacity of 169MW. 
 
During a power outage event, the first back up (or remediation) is that of the dual redundant power 
supply. If this also suffers a failure, the second back up is to utilise the generators to power the site. 
Essentially, the back -up generators serve as a se condary back -up to the primary provision of a dual 
redundant power supply, and as such their use is designed to be minimal. 
 
Twelve (12) above ground bulk fuel tanks will be located on basement level of the generator gantry to 
store diesel along with associated pump sets. Each plantroom contains six (6) generators.  
 
The office/FOH generator will be housed in an acoustically treated enclosure and mounted on the roof 
of the office/FOH building. One (1) above ground bulk fuel tank will be located in the basement level in 
a dedicated tank room to store diesel along with a ssociated pump set. Fuel tanks will be designed to 
comply with AS 1940.   
 
The generators will each be tested to ensure they are functional in the event of a utility failure. Initially 
all generators as they are installed at the Site will undergo commission testing which involves several 
hours that the generator will run. After the generator is installed and commissioned, regular testing will 
occur for all generators. For a breakdown of generator testing across the Site, refer to TABLE 11 below. 
 
TABLE 11. GENERATOR TESTING REQUIREMENTS 
Parameter Proposed 
Total number of generators 73 (72 x IT, 1 x FOH) 
Testing frequency Monthly (12 testing periods) 
Operational testing hours Daytime hours (7:00am to 6:00pm Mondays to Sundays 
Generators tested simultaneously Two (one in eastern gantry, one in western gantry) 
Tests per period 36.5 
Duration per test 27 minutes total (22 mins run time, 5 mins cool down) 
Total testing time per period 985.5 minutes 
Total testing time per year 11,826 minutes 
Total testing hours per year 197.1 hours 
 
Generators are tested by connecting to a load bank (equipment that consumes electricity for testing 
purposes). Only one generator per gantry wing would be tested at any one time (two in total) with two 
(2) 3MW load banks being proposed on the roof of the main building to use for UPS and generator 
testing. Two (2) generators are proposed to run standard t ests at the same time to reduce the total 
testing time of all generators per annum. 
 
  
[[page 55]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
 P a g e  42 
3.3.10.2 Cooling System 
 
The Proposal seeks to provide a water-cooled chiller system for the data halls , utilising open circuit 
cooling towers (CTs), water cooled chillers, and fan wall units (FWUs).  
 
Chilled water is produced by water -cooled chillers and heat is rejected via open circuit cooling towers 
to the atmosphere. The chilled water produced is pumped to serve fan wall units which supply cool air 
into the data halls and remove heat from the data halls. Water side free cooling is possible using plate 
heat exchangers and this will reduce the operation hours of the chiller resulting in improved annualised 
PUE. Water is required for the cooling tower operation. 
 
3.3.11 Staging 
 
The approval strategy seeks to obtain development consent to complete the construction works over a 
single construction stage upon issue of the relevant Construction Certificates . Notwithstanding, the 
internal data halls will be progressively fitted out during the lifecycle of the data centre. 
 
A program has been established for the completion of the various work with a maximum duration of  
238 weeks. The various phases are as follows:  
  
• Earthworks and Site preparation 30 weeks  
• Construction and fitout    208 weeks 
3.4 PLANNING AGREEMENT 
The Proposal seeks to rely on the increased building height allowable under Clause 7.7 of RLEP 2014 in 
addition to a Clause 4.6 Variation Request for the exceedance caused by the roof plant and mesh. Clause 
7.7 allows increased height if the consent authority is satisfied that the development includes adequate 
provision for one or both of: 
 
• Recreation areas that are configured and located in a way that is appropriate for the 
recreational purposes of Macquarie Park; or 
• An access network that is configured and located in a way that will allow a suitable level of 
connectivity within the Precinct. 
 
The Proposal satisfies the above requirements with the through-site links providing an access network 
and recreations areas on Site. The EIS is accompanied by a Draft Letter of Offer to enter into a Planning 
Agreement (refer Appendix 31), which will be negotiated between the Proponent and the City of Ryde 
Council that ensures these measures are provided. 
 
This matter is discussed in more detail in Section 6.1.24 of this EIS.

3.5 PROJECT NEED  
The intention of the Proposal is to construct and operate a data centre building containing 12 x data 
halls and associated infrastructure and amenities which would provide a secure, adaptable, and 
sustainable facility within the Macquarie Park Technology Precinct.  
 
Designed to meet Tier III Uptime Institute standards, the data centre will provide high -performance 
infrastructure that ensures continuous operations while adapting to evolving technological needs. The 
project is committed to aligning with local planning r egulations and incorporating environmentally 
responsible design.  
 
A key priority is future proofing, ensuring the facility can scale efficiently to accommodate increasing 
data demands. Security and reliability are integral, with redundant power and cooling systems, 
advanced cybersecurity, and robust access controls.   
 
From a technical standpoint, the facility’s design will follow industry best practices, incorporating 
structurally robust data halls, energy -efficient cooling systems, secure perimeters, and fire -resistant 
materials. The layout will emphasize operational e fficiency, featuring modular data halls, structured 
cabling, emergency exits, and designated areas for storage, security, and support services.  
 
The Proposal will be a cutting-edge facility that integrates innovation, security, and sustainability.  
 
The Australian Government has identified cyber security as one of the key growth industry sectors. The 
cyber security sector is forecast to have an annual cyber security revenue growth of 5.5% by 2026. Cyber 
Security Growth Centre, AustCyber, has prepared  Australia’s Cyber Security Sector Competitiveness 
Plan 2022 (Sector Competitiveness Plan). The Sector Competitiveness Plan notes three key actions to 
grow revenue for the Australian cyber security sector. The Proposal is aligned with key action 2: Bolster 
domestic procurement and export capacity. The Proposal would assist in growing the domestic cyber 
security market and facilitating increased export capacity. 
 
Data centres are critical infrastructure for supporting secure regional and national government and 
business and provides employment-generating opportunities to the Ryde LGA and wider Sydney area.  
 
Additionally, the Proposal would generate the following benefits: 
 
▪ Supporting a distributed work model and the ability to ‘work form anywhere’. Liveability is 
benefited from enabling digital infrastructure that extends beyond the precinct, supporting people 
to work flexibly if it suits their circumstances. 
▪ Reduced travel distances, leading to savings in time and fuel for local working residents, due to 
much better access to the Site, as opposed to other dense employment areas at the local level. It 
is noted that a reduction in travel times and distances generates related benefits, including 
reduced vehicle wear and tear, reduced fuel costs, reduced pollution, reduced traffic congestion, 
reduced risks of car accidents and more time that can be spent either wo rking, socialising or 
undertaking other activities. 
▪ Job creation near homes and consequent economic multiplier impacts, which boosts the local 
economy. 
3.6 CONSIDERATION OF ALTERNATIVES  
The purpose of the proposed development is to provide a data centre offering, that responds to  the 
intended character and nature of the E3 Productivity Support zone.  
 
  
[[page 67]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde 
  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  54  
The Proposal seeks to ensure:  
 
▪ It is compatible with surrounding development and the local context;   
▪ It would provide development of an otherwise undeveloped land holding;   
▪ It would result in minimal impact on the environment; and  
▪ It would allow for the implementation of suitable mitigation measures, where required. 
 
Overall, the scale of the Proposal is considered suitable, and the built form proposed would completely 
enhance and renew an u ndeveloped land portion into a modernised  data centre offering, which 
evolves as a logical extension to the evolving Macquarie Park Precinct. The Site design and layout of the 
built form proposed seeks to maintain consistency with the zone objectives of RLEP 2014 and enhance 
the underlying desired future character for the Site.  
 
The options considered and subsequently dismissed, in arriving to the current proposal with regard to 
the Proposal included:  
 
(a) ‘Do Nothing’ Scenario 
 
This option was dismissed as the objectives of the Proposal would not be met. If the proposed 
development was not to proceed, the Site would not achieve its intended development outcome and 
would not satisfy the significant demand for data storage . Based on the above and the justification 
provided within Section 3.5, the ‘do nothing’ scenario is dismissed.  
 
(b) Development on an Alternative Site 
 
Consideration was given to carrying out development on alternate sites, these were dismissed as the 
Site resulted in the most beneficial outcomes for the Proposal. Beneficial characteristics of the Site for 
the Proposal include:  
 
▪ It’s context, being subject to the provisions of the E3 Productivity Support zone pursuant to the 
provisions of RLEP 2014 and the consistency with the surrounding commercial area; 
▪ All potential environmental impacts concerning the proposed development are generally able 
to be suitably mitigated within the Site;  
▪ The proximity to the regional road network provides accessibility and linkages to the broader 
area and wider regions;  
▪ The capability for providing employment-generating opportunities (both directly and 
indirectly), during both the construction and operational phases; and 
▪ The Site can be developed with appropriate visual amenity achieved given its surrounding 
context. 
 
(c) Different Site Configuration  
 
The configuration of the Proposal was chosen based on the characteristics of the Site, including the 
desire to utilise the existing excavation, avoid tree removal and retain  existing native vegetation, the 
requirements of the Macquarie Park Design Guide including the required new street and through-site 
links, and the requirements of the end user, as well as the need to respond to the character of the 
surrounding area. It is noted that a different site configuration would not have been able to respond to 
the abovementioned site opportunities and constraints. The proposed Site configuration was therefore 
considered to be the most appropriate. The design options considered are discussed in more detail in 
the Design Report provided at Appendix 3 of this EIS.  
 
Notwithstanding, the Proposal is justified on the basis that it is compatible with the locality in which it 
is proposed, resulting in positive social and economic benefits, whilst appropriately managing and 
mitigating any potential environmental impacts requiring consideration.

In accordance with Schedule 1 of the Planning Systems SEPP,

properties,

destination of any excavated material,

(vii) the proximity to, and potential for 
adverse impacts on, any waterway, 
drinking water catchment or 
environmentally sensitive area,

Clause 6.6   Environmental sustainability 
Pursuant to Clause 6.6(2) of the LEP,

to development on land in an employment

metres in gross floor area or greater unless 
the consent authority is satisfied that the 
development has regard to the following— 
(a) water demand reduction, including 
water efficiency, water recycling and 
minimisation of potable water usage, 
(b) energy demand reduction, including 
energy generation, use of renewable 
energy and reduced reliance on mains 
power, 
(c) indoor environmental quality, including 
daylight provision, glare control, 
increased outside air rates, thermal 
comfort, 
(d) a reduction in new materials 
consumption and use of sustainable 
materials, including recycled content in 
concrete, sustainable timber and PVC 
minimisation, 
(e) emissions reduction, including reduced 
flow to sewer and light pollution, 
(f) transport initiatives to reduce car 
dependence such as providing cycle 
facilities, car share and small vehicle 
parking spaces, 
(g) land use and ecology, including 
reduced topsoil removal and 
contaminated land reclamation.

6.1.3 Design Quality 
 
6.1.3.1 Better Placed 
 
The Proposal achieves good design in accordance with the seven (7) objectives for good design in Better 
Placed, as addressed below and documented in the Design Report prepared by Greenbox Architecture 
at Appendix 3 of this EIS. 
 
Objective 1 - Better Fit - Contextual, local and of its place 
 
The design responds carefully to the Site’s characteristics, including its topography and surrounding 
land use. By positioning the building in a manner that minimises its height and visual impact, the 
design ensures minimal disruption to the local environm ent. The surrounding native vegetation is 
preserved to maintain ecological value and contribute to local biodiversity.  
 
The integration of an east-west access street improves circulation and connectivity, linking key access 
points and providing better access for both pedestrians and vehicles. This ensures clear separation 
between operational and public zones, complementing the surrounding employment precinct. 
 
The ‘fit’ of the Proposal is further articulated through the visual analysis provided within Section 6.1.4 
of this EIS. 
 
Objective 2 - Better Performance - Sustainable, adaptable and durable 
 
The data centre is designed to meet high-performance standards, including compliance with Uptime 
Institute Tier III standards, ensuring continuous operation with redundant power and cooling systems. 
This guarantees secure data processing while adapting to increasing demands over time. Energy -
efficient technologies and water -saving systems reduce the environmental footprint and operational 
costs.  
 
Durability is ensured through robust materials and high -performance mechanical and electrical 
systems, optimised for data centre use. The design also incorporates resource optimisation and waste 
minimisation, making it environmentally responsible and economically viable in the long term. 
 
The Proposal will incorporate several Ecologically Sustainable Development (ESD) principles,  as 
detailed within Section 6.1.8 of this EIS. 
 
Objective 3 - Better for Community - Inclusive, connected and diverse 
  
The design prioritises positive impacts on the surrounding community by enhancing the local 
streetscape and improving accessibility. Landscaping along the building’s perimeter softens its 
appearance, improving the visual amenity of the surrounding area. Th e integration of an east -west 
access street facilitates better pedestrian and vehicular movement across the site.  
 
Landscaped zones and outdoor seating areas create spaces for relaxation and public interaction, 
contributing to the local community’s overall experience. This thoughtful design ensures the data 
centre is more integrated with the broader environment, benefiting both workers and the public 
 
  
[[page 82]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde 
  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  69  
Objective 4 - Better for People - Safe, comfortable and liveable 
 
The design focuses on creating a comfortable and engaging environment for staff and visitors. Office 
and administration areas face outward, maximising solar access and natural light, promoting a 
healthier work environment. Extensive glazing allows for visu al connection with the surrounding 
environment, encouraging openness and enhancing the interior experience.  
 
The dynamic entry and clear signage improve navigation, ensuring that both staff and visitors can easily 
find their way around. Breakout areas in landscaped zones provide informal meeting spaces, 
contributing to employee wellbeing and fostering a welcoming environment 
 
The following design treatments have been adopted to address Objective 3: 
 
• There will be general lighting to the entry point and car parking areas that is located to the 
southern side of the Site. This should not adversely increase light spill or affect nearby visual 
receives.  
• Acoustic screening is featured around the plant equipment on roof to reduce and mitigate 
noise pollution. 
 
Objective 5 - Better Working - Functional, efficient and fit for purpose 
 
Functionality is central to the data centre’s design, ensuring operational efficiency and security. The 
facility supports modular data halls for scalability, with redundant power and cooling systems to 
guarantee reliability. The layout clearly separates pu blic, staff, and operational areas, maintaining 
security while facilitating a smooth flow of people and resources.  
 
The integration of an east -west access street enhances site circulation, providing clear routes for 
pedestrians and vehicles while reducing disruptions. The design ensures that the data centre’s core 
operations align with the broader context of the employment precinct, creating a safe and efficient 
environment. 
 
Objective 6 - Better Value - Creating and adding value 
 
The design delivers long-term value through energy-efficient technologies and the selection of durable 
materials, reducing operational costs and environmental impacts. The use of low-maintenance systems 
and high  performance mechanical and electrical features ensures that the facility will remain 
functional and economically viable for years. 
 
In addition, compliance with Tier III standards guarantees operational efficiency and scalability. This 
long-term value extends to the surrounding area, contributing to the area’s ongoing development. 
 
Objective 7 – Better Look and Feel 
 
The visual quality of the data centre is enhanced through careful design, using natural materials and a 
restrained colour palette that allows the structure to blend into the existing urban fabric. Landscaping 
around the building softens its appearance and contributes to the overall aesthetic while improving 
ecological quality.  
 
Dynamic entry points and outdoor seating areas improve the user experience, making the data centre 
feel welcoming and connected to the community. These features ensure that the development 
enhances the visual and functional quality of the surrounding precinct while maintaining a strong 
identity.

ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde 
  SSD-80018208
