[[page 195]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  182  
The systems and equipment associated with the mechanical services supporting the IT load includes: 
 
• Water cooled chillers, open circuit cooling towers, plate heat exchangers, make up water tanks 
and associated chilled/condenser water pumps 
• Associated pipework and system ancillaries 
• Data hall pressurisation units (PAHUs) 
• Chilled Water Fan wall units (FWUs) 
• Chilled water CRAH units for electrical rooms 
• Air cooled DX FCUs for HV Incomer and main comms rooms 
 
Chilled water will be produced by a series of high efficiency, water cooled, electric vapour compression 
HFO chillers. Chilled water is then supplied to Fan Wall Units within each data hall providing cooling to 
the Data Hall IT load. Chillers will be speci fied to operate using refrigerant with zero ozone depletion 
potential. Heat produced by the chillers will be rejected to atmosphere by a condenser water system, 
a number of dedicated open loop cooling towers. Cooling towers will be selected to ensure high 
performance operation in low load conditions to enhance energy and water efficiency.  
 
The design includes for water side economiser (heat exchangers) whereby condenser water can either 
precool the chilled water return before entering the chiller or fully by-pass the chiller and solely depend 
on low condenser water temperature to meet the data hall cooling requirement during periods when 
ambient conditions permit condenser water supply temperature to be below required chilled water 
temperature (free cooling mode).  
 
If the chilled water supply temperature can be increased to a point that it is warmer than the condenser 
water flow temperature including a provision for plate heat exchanger inefficiency, then free cooling 
can be relied upon through the whole year and no chiller is required. 
 
Cooling to the Data Hall IT load will utilise full air recirculation Fan Wall Units (FWU) connected to the 
dedicated chilled water circuit serving the associated data hall. FWU will provide cooling to the cold 
aisle and return air via hot aisle and overhead return plenum. 
 
Alternative Cooling Options  
 
This section summarises the evaluation of cooling solution options reviewed for the Proposal.  The 
following technical requirements were considered when conducting the analysis: 
 
• A data hall supply air temperature of 23°C 
• A low Power Usage Effectiveness (PUE) 
• Ability to be adapted to suit various tenants 
• Flexibility for design to be modified to suit customer needs 
• Noise emitted  
 
The current design trends faced by the data centre industry is the adoption of direct -to-chip cooling. 
Liquid to chip cooling has gradually been deployed in some data centres in Australia. Another 
noticeable trend in data centre technology is the rise in temperature within cold aisles which allows 
the chilled water temperature to be raised and potentially the removal of chillers if water cooled 
solutions are used. The cooling solution chosen must be flexible in taking on these trends  
 
The following cooling options have been reviewed during the concept design: 
 
• Option 1: Air-cooled chillers + Fan Wall Units (FWUs) 
• Option 2: Open circuit cooling towers (CTs) + water cooled chillers + FWUs 
• Option 3: Hybrid towers + water cooled chillers + FWUs 
• Option 4: Open circuit CTs + direct expansion (DX) FWU/Computer Room Air Conditioning 
(CRAC) units 
[[page 196]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  183  
These systems represent the most common cooling solutions used in Australia for data centre 
applications. Each listed option includes a description of its operational functionality. 
 
A discussion about the feasibility of each of the above cooling options is provided in the Infrastructure 
Delivery, Management and Staging Plan provided at Appendix 29. In conclusion, Option 2 with its used 
of open circuit cooling towers and water-cooled chillers to provide cooling to the data centre is shown 
to be the most efficient solution. The option is flexible in its design, and it can also be adapted to liquid 
to chip cooling which will make the cooling solution even more efficient. It has lowest noise emission 
which is important for site close to residential towers 
 
6.1.22 Bush Fire Risk  
 
As demonstrated in Figure 89 below, the Site is identified as containing Category 1 Vegetation and 
Vegetation Buffer land and is therefore bush fire prone land. 
 
A Bushfire Assessment (BA) has been prepared by Bushfire Hazard Solutions in support of the Proposal 
and is provided at Appendix 30. 
 
The Proposal is categorised by the NSW Rural Fire Service (NSW RFS) planning policy document 
Planning for Bush Fire Protection 2019  (PBP) as ‘other non -residential development’, and more 
specifically ‘commercial and industrial development’.  
 
For this type of development, the NSW RFS requires that development applications should satisfy the 
aims and objectives of PBP . The BA also considered  specific objectives and bushfire protection 
measures (BPMs) detailed in Chapter 8 ‘Other Development’ of PBP , including APZs, Access, Services , 
and Emergency Management Plan.  
 
Consideration has also been given to section 8.3.1 ‘Buildings of Class 5 to 8 under the NCC’ of PBP.

6.1.22.1 Recommended Bushfire Protection measures 
 
The intent of bushfire protection measures for this development is to minimise the risk of bush fire 
attack and provide protection for emergency services personnel, residents and others assisting 
firefighting activities.  
 
The following recommendations are provided as the minimum necessary for compliance with PBP. 
Additional recommendations are provided to supplement these minimum requirements where 
considered necessary. 
 
Asset Protection Zones 
 
1. At the commencement of construction works and in perpetuity all areas within the subject 
property to the northern, eastern and western boundaries and for a minimum of 20 metres to 
the south of the Data Centre shall be maintained as an Asset Protection Zone (Inner Protection 
Area) as detailed in the NSW Rural Fire Service’s document ‘Standards for Asset Protection 
Zones’ and Appendix 4 of Planning for Bush Fire Protection 2019 
 
Construction 
 
2. That all gaps >3mm or more on proposed buildings be either screened within aluminium, steel 
or bronze metal mesh having an aperture size of ≤2mm or be sealed / closed.  
3. That all operable windows be screened internally or externally with aluminium, steel or bronze 
metal mesh having an aperture size of ≤2mm in such a way that the entire opening is screened 
when in the open position. 
4. That the external side-hung doors on proposed buildings be tight fitting and fitted with a draft 
excluder. This may require draft excluders on the stiles, head, sill or threshold and rebated or 
planted jambs & centre stiles. 
5. That the external roller doors on proposed buildings have tight fitting guide tracks / seals (not 
providing a gap >3mm when in the closed position).  
6. That any mechanical ducted ventilation on proposed buildings provides screens over air intake 
vents and ensuring that exhaust vents are either screened or fitted with dampers that close 
when positive outward air pressure is lost to ensure the maintain ember protection to the 
inside of the buildings. 
7. External walls are non -combustible, and the building structure will have a minimum 2 -hour 
fire rating.   
 
Landscaping 
 
8. That any new landscaping within the Asset Protection Zones is to comply with Table 7.4a of 
Planning for Bush Fire Protection 2019 
 
Gas (where applicable) 
 
9. That any new gas services are to comply with Table 7.4a of Planning for Bush Fire Protection 
2019 as follows: 
• reticulated or bottled gas is installed and maintained in accordance with AS/NZS 
1596:2014 and the requirements of relevant authorities, and metal piping is used; 
• all fixed gas cylinders are kept clear of all flammable materials to a distance of 10m and 
shielded on the hazard side;  
• connections to and from gas cylinders are metal;  
• polymer-sheathed flexible gas supply lines are not used; and  
• above-ground gas service pipes are metal, including and up to any outlets. 
 
  
[[page 198]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  185  
Emergency Management 
 
10. That the bushfire emergency management plan to be prepared and is to be consistent with 
the NSW Rural Fire Service Guidelines for the Preparation of Emergency / Evacuation Plan. 
 
Water 
 
11. That the new hydrant system is to comply with the requirements detailed in Table 5.3c of 
Planning for Bush Fire Protection 2019, specifically: 
• fire hydrant spacing, design and sizing comply with the relevant clauses of AS 2419.1:2021; 
• hydrants are not located within any road carriageway; 
• fire hydrant flows and pressures comply with the relevant clauses of AS 2419.1:2021. 
• all above-ground water service pipes external to the building are metal, including and up 
to any taps. 
• fire hose reels are constructed in accordance with AS/NZS 1221:1997 Fire hose reels, and 
installed in accordance with the relevant clauses of AS 2441:2005 Installation of fire hose 
reels. 
 
Access 
 
12. That any new access roads comply with the following requirements for Property Access as 
detailed in section 7.4 of Planning for Bush Fire Protection 2019: 
• property access roads are two-wheel drive, all-weather roads 
• a minimum vertical clearance of 4m to any overhanging obstructions, including tree 
branches;  
• curves have a minimum inner radius of 6m and are minimal in number to allow for rapid 
access and egress;  
• the minimum distance between inner and outer curves is 6m;  
• the crossfall is not more than 10 degrees;  
• maximum grades for sealed roads do not exceed 15 degrees and not more than 10 degrees 
for unsealed roads. 
 
In accordance with the bushfire safety measures contained within the BA , and consideration of the 
Site-specific bushfire risk assessment, the BA  concludes that when combined, they will provide a 
reasonable and satisfactory level of bushfire protection to the Proposal and  satisfies all relevant 
specifications and requirements of PBP. 
 
The Proposal must be carried out in accordance with the above recommendations which  are 
documented in planned management and mitigation measures described in Appendix D of this EIS. 
 
Subject to the BPMs being incorporated into the Proposal, the BDA co ncludes that the Proposal is 
Complies with the requirements of PBP.

6.1.24 Contributions and Public Benefit  
 
6.1.24.1 Local Infrastructure Contributions 
 
The Macquarie Park Corridor Section 7.12 Local Infrastructure Contributions Plan 2025 applies to the 
Site and the Proposal  under Section 7.13 of the EP&A Act. A S7.12 levy is payable based on the EDC of 
the Proposal, which is calculated to be $1,582,669,210 (exc. GST). The applicable levy is 1.5% of the EDC 
of the Proposal.  
 
Contribution rates are indexed quarterly and an appropriate rate will be a calculated as part of any 
consent granted for the Proposal.  
 
6.1.24.2 Housing and Productivity Contributions 
 
Regional contributions are levied under Division 7.1, Subdivision 4 of the EP&A Act. The Site is located 
within the Greater Sydney Region and the Proposal is a n industrial development and therefore, the 
applicable contributions rate is: 
 
$15 per square meter of new GFA for industrial development 
 
It is noted that the above rate is indexed quarterly, and an appropriate rate will be a calculated as part 
of any consent granted for the Proposal. 
 
6.1.24.3 Planning Agreement 
 
The Proposal seeks to rely on the increased building height allowable under Clause 7.7 of RLEP 2014 in 
addition to a Clause 4.6 Variation Request for the exceedance caused by the roof plant and mesh. 
Clause 7.7 allows increased height if the consent authority is satisfied that the development includes 
adequate provision for one or both of: 
 
• Recreation areas that are configured and located in a way that is appropriate for the 
recreational purposes of Macquarie Park; or 
• An access network that is configured and located in a way that will allow a suitable level of 
connectivity within the Precinct. 
 
The objective of Clause 7.7 is to encourage additional commercial development in the Macquarie Park 
Transport Oriented Development Precinct coordinated with an adequate access network and 
recreation areas. 
 
The Proposal satisfies the above requirements with the through-site links providing an access network 
and recreations areas on Site. The EIS is accompanied by a Draft Letter of Offer to enter into a Planning 
Agreement (refer Appendix 31), which will be negotiated between the Proponent and the City of Ryde 
Council that ensures these measures are provided. 
 
Public benefit infrastructure in accordance with the provisions of Clause 7.7 of RLEP 2014  and the 
Macquarie Park Design Guide is proposed to be provided in the following manner, which will be offset 
against the contribution rate. 
 
• A public through-site link along the north-eastern boundary of the Site extending from Julius 
Avenue to Richardson Place. A public recreation area and lookout area is provided along this 
link that will provide passive recreation for workers of nearby buildings and residents; 
• A public through-site link along the south-western boundary of the Site extending from Julius 
Avenue to the new street and the bushwalking tracks; 
• Footpaths on either side of the new street; 
• Upgraded and extended through-site link from Richardson Place to the bushwalking tracks on 
the south-eastern boundary of the Site; and  
[[page 200]]

engagement, aligned with the IAP2 Public Participation Spectrum for Engagement. The following 
hierarchy of engagement is proposed that is appropriate to the scale of the Proposal and has been 
informed by the analysis of the Proposal, context and stakeholder analysis. This involves:  
  
• Tier 1: Informing, consulting with and involving NSW DPHI, relevant agencies and authorities, 
City of Ryde Council and Aboriginal Community,   
• Teir 2: Informing and consulting /interviews with adjoining neighbours and 
resident/community groups, local businesses/institutions, and 
• Tier 3: Informing and consulting with the surrounding local business and residential 
communities.

• Dedicated Email 
 
These tools are discussed in more detail in the Engagement Report.

concern including trees, bushland and walking trails, new traffic, visual impacts, stormwater and

Conclusion and next steps 
 
Consideration of agency engagements did not identify any significant concerns and confirmed where  
ongoing engagement is required by the proponent following approval, particularly in relation to 
utilities.  
 
The community stakeholder engagement reach was widespread, with 1,200 Community Newsletter 
distributed to the surrounding businesses and residential areas. However, the community interest in 
the proposal was low, with less than five survey results from the  general community and direct 
communication with two of the ten local community / interest groups. Key themes related to the 
natural settings and role of substation.  
 
[[page 207]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  194  
Agency engagement was limited as guidelines for the preparation of technical reports provided the 
necessary guidance to address agency considerations. Where required engagement on a range of 
relevant technical matters was undertaken including with councils  and utility providers. Whilst 
community interest was low, there was direct engagement with key stakeholders including immediate 
neighbours and Lane Cove National Park and adjoining councils.   
 
Detailed feedback on the proposal was provided by:   
 
• City of Ryde Council including comprehensive Pre-EIS lodgement engagement, use of 
incentive heights and alignment with the Macquarie Park Design Guide including new link 
road and new through-site eastern and western pedestrian links to walking trails;  
• Ausgrid as part of the inclusion of the STSS assessed as part of the Proposal;  
• Aboriginal Community as part of the ACHAR, including connection to Country elements; and  
• Neighbouring properties, in particular the eastern and western commercial property owners 
in relation to vehicle and pedestrian links as well as National Parks and Wildlife Service in 
relation to the interface with the Lane Cove National Park to the south of the Site.  
 
It is noted that the community stakeholders have all recently been engaged on the planning 
framework for the wider area as part of updates to the Neighbourhood Master Plan. This was 
undertaken as the Riverside Corporate Park area is located in the Macquarie Park accelerated Transport 
Orientated Development area. It is further noted that the local area had previously been engaged as 
part of the assessment process for the 2009 approval for four commercial towers on the Site.  
 
The Proponent continues to work closely with Ausgrid on delivery considerations for the proposed 
substation. The consideration of agency and utility requirements and engagements did not identify 
any significant concerns and confirmed where ongoing engagement is  required by the proponent 
following approval, particularly in relation to utilities and interface with Lane Cove National Park.   
 
The key concerns identified through engagement related to the natural surroundings and the impact 
to existing vegetation, integration with the surrounding development and operational matters 
including traffic flows. Consideration of these stakeholders have  been incorporated into the detailed 
proposal refined for the EIS submission including new road link design, stormwater, design response 
to create visual interest and commercial streetscape and the landscape design including retention of 
native bushland an d improvements to walking trails and new pedestrian links on the site. The EIS 
assessment process will allow for further engagement of this evolution of the proposal.  
 
The Proponent also has an undertaking for ongoing engagement requirements with Aboriginal 
community members, two community groups and the four community stakeholders that responded  
to the survey.  
 
In conclusion, the Engagement Report confirms that an appropriate level of engagement has been 
undertaken, and there is an appropriate response to concerns raised through engagement.  In this 
context, the Proposal is considered suitable for progressing to a detailed assessment stage. 
 
  
[[page 208]]

6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208

6.2 CUMULATIVE ASSESSMENT 
 
This section of the EIS assesses whether the Proposal is likely to generate cumulative impacts with the 
projects identified in accordance with the Department’s Cumulative Impact Guidelines. 
 
Relevant cumulative impact assessments are summarised below.  
 
6.2.1 Cumulative Air Quality Impact 
 
The AQIA prepared for the Proposal at Appendix 17 confirms that predicted cumulative concentrations 
for a realistic emissions scenario during routine maintenance of the back -up generators show that no 
exceedances are predicted to occur at any surrounding receptors for all assessed pollutants. The 
predicted cumulative concentrations for all assessed pollutants remain below the relevant criteria 
under realistic operations, w here the standby generators are to be operated under an appropriate 
testing schedule. 
 
Therefore, the cumulative impacts of the Proposal in respect of air quality are considered acceptable.  
 
6.2.2 Cumulative Traffic Impact 
 
The TAIA provided at Appendix 8  provides a cumulative assessment of the traffic impacts of the 
Proposal in a future year 2036 scenario The assessment indicated that all surrounding intersections will 
only experience marginal increases in traffic as a result of the Proposal. Given the low rates of traffic 
generation of the development  and minimal parking provided , especially compared against a 
commercial develop of the same scale, the cumulative impact of the Proposal is minimal.  
 
6.2.3 Cumulative Noise and Vibration 
 
The NVIA prepared for the Proposal  at Appendix 18 indicates that there are minimal industrial noise 
generating activities in the vicinity of the Site and therefore the cumulative impact of the Proposal is 
minimal in respect of noise and vibration generation. A cumulative assessment is provided in the NVIA 
provided at Appendix 18. 
 
6.2.4 Cumulative GHG Emissions 
 
The development contributes to cumulative GHG emissions in the area which is characterised by 
numerous energy-intensive data centres. Notwithstanding, the ESD Report prepared for the Proposal 
at Appendix outlines sustainable design features and energy efficiency measures to reduce it’s GHG 
footprint.  
 
6.3 PLANNED MANAGEMENT AND MITIGATION MEASURES  
 
The Proponent  plans to undertake the construction and operation of the proposed data centre , in 
accordance with the planned management and mitigation measures  outlined within Appendix D of 
this EIS.

PART 7 EVALUATION 
This section of the EIS provides a comprehensive evaluation of the Proposal with regard to its economic, 
environmental, and social impacts, including the principles of ESD. It assesses the potential benefits 
and impacts of the Proposal, considering the int eraction between the findings in the detailed 
assessments and the compliance of the Proposal with the relevant controls and policies.  
 
Various components of the biophysical, social, and economic environments, as well as the Proposal’s 
alignment with the objects of the EP&A Act and other statutory instruments applicable to the Site, have 
been examined in this EIS and are summarised below 
7.1 ECOLOGICALLY SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT 
The four (4) principles of ESD as outlined in Clause 193 of the EP&A Regulation have been carefully 
considered in the formulation of the Proposal. An analysis of these principles is provided  in Section 
6.1.8.1 of this EIS. 
7.2 STRATEGIC CONTEXT 
The Site and Proposal contribute to the objectives set out in the Greater Sydney Region Plan  – A 
Metropolis of Three Cities by promotion of technological advancements and employment-generating 
opportunities to the wider locality and community, positioned within the Macquarie Park Precinct.   
 
The Proposal is consistent with the 10 Directions and planning priorities described in the Region Plan, 
specifically in the following manner: 
 
▪ Infrastructure and collaboration – once in operation, the Proposal would be able to provide 
secure, reliable and adaptable solutions for cloud, content and large enterprise customers to 
house their high and rapidly growing volumes of data and information. The infrastructure has 
been designed to meet future needs. 
▪ Liveability – the Proposal encourages employment -generating opportunities and economic 
prosperity, which has positive influences on the wider locality.  
▪ Productivity – the Proposal is situated within the North District Plan  and will support the 
retention and efficient use of employment land; and,   
▪ Sustainability – the Proposal would not exhibit or emit any detrimental impacts to its wider 
ecological surroundings and is consistent with the principles of ESD.   
 
The Proposal is aligned within the Planning Priorities for the Ryde LGA as outlined in the Ryde Local 
Strategic Planning Statement. The Proposal seeks to establish a new data centre on the Site which 
includes increases the density of the employment land resulting in minimal additional vehicular 
movements, which is consistent with Council’s vision for the area as outline in the LSPS.  
 
Data centres are critical infrastructure for supporting secure regional and national government and 
businesses. Additionally, it offers several advantages, including facilitating a distributed work model, 
reducing travel distances and associated costs for local workers, creating new employment 
opportunities, and contributing to the local economy through job creation near residential areas. 
7,3 STATUTORY CONTEXT 
The relevant State and local environmental planning instruments are identified at PART 4 of this EIS 
and assessed at Appendix B. This assessment has demonstrated that the Proposal will satisfy the 
applicable provisions of statutory planning instruments that apply to the Site. This is summarised 
below:

Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  197  
▪ The Proposal has been assessed and designed to satisfy the relevant objectives of the EP&A 
Act; 
▪ This EIS has been prepared in accordance with the SEARs as required by Schedule 2 of the 
EP&A Regulations. Refer to Appendix A; 
▪ Consideration is given to the relevant matters for consideration as required under the BC Act. 
The submission of a BDAR has been provided to support the Proposal; 
▪ The Proposal is consistent with the objectives of the E3 Productivity Support zone pursuant to 
RLEP 2014; 
▪ The Proposal satisfies the relevant provisions of the RLEP 2014, notwithstanding the proposed 
building height variation, as discussed below; 
▪ The Proposal is consistent with the provisions of the Macquarie Park Design Guide and relevant 
provisions of RDCP 2014; and 
▪ The Proposal is consistent with all statutory requirements as outlined in relevant SEPPs.  
 
Proposed Height Variation 
 
The Proposal seeks to vary the maximum incentive building height development standard applicable 
to the Site, as prescribed by Clause 7.7 of RLEP 2014. The Proposal is supported by a Clause 4.6 Variation 
Request which is provided at Appendix 34. The Variation Request establishes environmental planning 
grounds for the variations sought and should be reviewed in conjunction with this EIS.  
 
It has been demonstrated that the Proposal satisfies the relevant provisions of Clause 4.6 of RLEP 2014, 
as well as the objectives of the incentive building height and FSR development standard and the 
objectives of the E3 zone, notwithstanding the proposed height variation. The arguments contained in 
the Clause 4.6 Variation Request are summarised in Section 0 of this EIS.  
7.4 EP&A REGULATION 
This EIS report has addressed the specification criteria within Section 190 and 192 of the EP&A 
Regulation. Similarly, Section 6.1.8 of the EIS has addressed the principles of ESD through the 
precautionary principle (and other considerations), which assesses the threats of any serious or 
irreversible environmental damage. No additional approvals will be necessary at this time to enable the 
Proposal, in accordance with Section 4.42 of the EP&A Act.  
7.5 LIKELY IMPACTS OF THE DEVELOPMENT 
Matters of environmental impact have been addressed extensively in PART 6 of this EIS. Where impacts 
are identified, this EIS and accompanying technical reports explain why they are acceptable and how 
they can be mitigated. The Proposal will not have significant impacts on any threatened flora or fauna 
species, or the impact on any nearby sensitive receivers by way of acoustic, air quality, or visual impacts.  
 
Overall, it has been determined that the proposal would have positive social impacts, whilst potential 
negative social impacts of the proposal can be managed through the implementation of appropriate 
mitigation measures. The overall social impacts of the Proposal as described in the SIA are likely to 
deliver benefits in relation to way of life, accessibility, surroundings, health, community, and culture. 
Based on the potential for residual impacts identified, mitigation me asures are recommended  
including the preparation of an EMP.  
 
In terms of economic impact, these may only be described as positive. The proposed use result in 
employment generation during the construction and occupation phases of the development and will 
provide broader economic impacts to the Ryde LGA by the economic investment in the area. 
 
  
[[page 211]]
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  198  
It is considered that the recommended mitigation measures in Appendix D will assist the consent 
authority in formulating conditions to be imposed to ensure that the environmental impacts of the 
Proposal are suitably managed. Therefore, the Proposal will not result in adverse impacts on the Site 
itself or the immediate locality as a result of its implementation. 
7.6 SUITABILITY OF THE SITE 
The Site’s consistency with applicable regional and local strategies is demonstrated in the 
comprehensive environmental assessment, provided in PART 6 of this EIS, which includes an analysis 
of all potential impacts, which has been informed by the relevant consultant reports. Accordingly, the 
environmental assessment prescribes recommendations and mitigation measures (where necessary), 
to account for all identified potential impacts, by the Proposal.   
 
Having regard to the characteristics of the Site and its location, the Proposal is suitable for the Site for 
the reasons below:   
 
▪ The site is zoned E3 Productivity Support which permits development for the purpose of data 
centres with consent. The Proposal is consistent with the objectives for the E3 Productivity 
Support zone.  
▪ The Site remains capable of being appropriately serviced to accommodate the Proposal.  
▪ The Site is well located in close proximity to arterial and major collector roads and will not have 
any adverse traffic impact on the area due to the low-traffic generation of the Proposal. 
▪ The Site is well-serviced by a range of public transport options that provide a direct connection 
to the CBD and surrounding suburbs. Access to suitable transportation options is an essential 
requirement for employment-generating uses.  
▪ The Site benefits from geographical separation from sensitive receivers and is suitably located 
within an established employment precinct.  
▪ The Site can appropriately accommodate the Proposal while balancing environmental and 
design consideration and preserving the amenity of neighbouring properties. 
▪ The architectural design of the Proposal will deliver a high quality and modern data centre 
development, 
▪ The Site will provide important infrastructure to service the local and regional area. 
 
The technical assessments prepared in support of the Proposal have concluded that the Site is suitable 
for the Proposal. 
7.7 PUBLIC INTEREST 
The Proposal is in the public interest as it: 
 
▪ Provides a significant employment -generating use within an established employment 
generating precinct and provides an important part of cloud infrastructure.  
▪ Provides additional trees to support local and regional targets to increase tree canopy 
coverage. 
▪ Is consistent with the ESD principles as defined by Section 190 of the EP&A Regulations. 
▪ Has undertaken an extensive program of engagement with relevant stakeholders predates the 
submission of this SSDA, as detailed at length within Appendix C and summarised in 0 of this 
EIS. 
▪ Provides significant investment in the industrial sector within the Ryde LGA that will contribute 
to increased livelihood outcomes associated with the construction and operation phases. This 
has the potential to positively support livelihoods, not just directly through job creation but 
also by extending to local businesses and the overall improvement of the precinct. 
▪ Provides public benefit infrastructure in accordance with the Macquarie Park Design Guide 
that improves connectivity and recreation for the public.

ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT STATEMENT 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
P a g e  199  
PART 8 CONCLUSION 
 
This EIS has been prepared to assess the environmental, social, and economic impacts of the proposed 
data centre development. The EIS has addressed the issues outlined in the SEARs and accords with 
Part 8 of the EP&A Regulation with regards to consideration of the relevant environmental planning 
instruments, built form, and social and environmental impacts resulting  from the Proposal. 
Appropriate mitigation measures have been identified to manage the impacts of the development 
through the construction and operational phases of the project.  
 
Having considered the detailed assessment undertaken in this EIS, the approval of this SSDA is 
recommended, subject to the implementation of suitable conditions of consent reflecting the 
recommended mitigation measures in Appendix D.

Pursuant to Clause 4.6 of the SEPP, the consent

carried out, and

made suitable for the purpose for which 
the development is proposed to be carried 
out, it is satisfied that the land will be 
remediated before the land is used for that 
purpose.

Ryde Local 
Environmental 
Plan 2014 
 
Clause 2.3 - Zone 
objectives and Land 
Use Table 
Pursuant to Clause 2.3(2) of the LEP, the consent 
authority must have regard to the objectives for 
development in a zone when determining a 
development application in respect of land 
within the zone.  
The objectives of the E3 zone are as follows: 
• To provide a range of facilities and services, 
light industries, warehouses and offices. 
• To provide for land uses that are compatible 
with, but do not compete with, land uses in 
surrounding local and commercial centres. 
• To maintain the economic viability of local 
and commercial centres by limiting certain 
retail and commercial activity. 
The Proposal is considered to be consistent with 
the objectives of the zone in the following 
manner: 
• The Proposal is for a data centre which is 
defined as a high technology industry, which 
is a sub definition of light industry. The 
Proposal is adjacent a range of office, 
commercial and light industrial uses and 
increases the range of development in the 
locality.  
• Data centres are permitted with consent in 
the E3 Productivity Support zone and 
therefore considered compatible with the 
surrounding development. The Proposal 
supports data intensive uses and does not 
compete with adjoining development.

• To provide for land uses that meet the needs 
of the community, businesses and industries 
but that are not suited to locations in other 
employment zones. 
• To provide opportunities for new and 
emerging light industries. 
• To enable other land uses that provide 
facilities and services to meet the day to day 
needs of workers, to sell goods of a large size, 
weight or quantity or to sell goods 
manufactured on-site. 
• To promote sustainable development, 
including public transport and working 
environments. 
• To encourage industries involved in research 
and development. 
• The Proposal will encourage employment 
and does not interfere with the viability of 
the Macquarie Park Accelerated TOD 
Precinct by limiting retail or commercial 
activity. 
• The need for data centres is continuously 
growing due to rapid technological 
advancements in the e-commerce sector. As 
such, the Proposal is considered to meet the 
needs of businesses and industries 
associated with the E3 Productivity Support 
zone.  
• Data centres are defined as high technology 
industry, which is a sub definition of light 
industry.  
• The Proposal promotes sustainable 
development through the retention of 
existing vegetation, upgrades to existing 
pathways, provision of recreational walkways 
to bushland, and being located within 600m 
of the North Ryde Metro Station. 
• Data centres are defined as high technology 
industry, which are driven by technological 
advancements in the e-commerce sector. As 
such, the Proposal is not inconsistent with 
this objective.

destruction of riparian vegetation or a 
A Flood Impact and Risk Assessment has been 
provided at Appendix 22 that has considered 
the compatibility of the Proposal with the flood 
function and behaviour on the land.  
The Flood Impact and Risk Assessment 
concludes the Proposal is demonstrated to have 
no adverse flood impacts on adjacent or 
downstream properties and the Site can be 
developed and operated safely, with appropriate 
design measures to manage residual flood risk, 
as discussed in Section 6.1.14 of this EIS. 
Considering this, the Proposal is considered to 
be consistent with Clause 5.21 of RLEP 2014.

of this clause.

the development,

redevelopment of the land, 
• The quality of any excavated fill or soil, and

MITIGATION MEASURES TABLE 
Julius Avenue Data Centre 
6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  SSD-80018208 
 
 
 
 
 
By: ISPT Pty Ltd 
In relation to: State Significant Development Application (SSD-67407231) 
For Julius Avenue Data Centre 
Site: 6-8 Julius Avenue, North Ryde  
LOT 89 DP 1082131 
 
ISPT Pty Ltd  plan to undertake the construction and operation of the proposed warehouse and 
distribution centre, in accordance with the following planned management and mitigation measures.  
 
PLANNED MANAGEMENT AND MITIGATION MEASURES FOR SSD-67407231 
ID Management / Mitigation Measure Timing 
Administrative Commitments  
A1 Commitment to Minimise Harm to the Environment 
The Proponent will commit to implement all reasonable and 
feasible measures, to prevent and/or minimise any harm to the 
environment, that may result from the construction or 
operation of the proposed development 
Prior to construction, 
during construction, 
and during operation. 
A2 Terms of Approval 
The Proponent will carry out the project generally in 
accordance with the: 
(a) Environmental Impact Statement;  
(b) Drawings;  
(c) Management and Mitigation Measures;   
(d) Any Conditions of Approval. 
If there is any inconsistency between the above, the Conditions 
of Approval shall prevail to the extent of the inconsistency.  
Prior to construction, 
during construction, 
and during operation. 
A3 Occupation Certificate  
The Proponent will ensure that Occupation Certificates are 
obtained prior to the occupation of the facilities. 
Prior to operation. 
A4 Compliance 
The Proponent will ensure compliance with any reasonable 
requirement(s) of the Secretary of the NSW DPHI arising from 
the assessment of:  
(a) Any reports, plans, programs, strategies or correspondence 
that are submitted in relation to this Approval; and  
(b) The implementation of any recommended actions or 
measures contained in reports, plans, programs, strategies 
or correspondence submitted by the Project Team as part 
of the application for Approval. 
Prior to construction, 
during construction, 
and during operation. 
A5 Structural Adequacy  
The Proponent will ensure that all new buildings and 
structures on the Site are constructed in accordance with the 
relevant requirements of the National Construction Code. 
During construction. 
A6 Construction Environmental Management Plan 
Prior to the commencement of construction, the Proponent  
would prepare a Construction Environmental Management 
Plan (CEMP) that addresses the following:  
Prior to construction.

(a) Air Quality;  
(b) Noise and Vibration; 
(c) Waste Classification;   
(d) Erosion and Sediment Control;  
(e) Materials Management Plan; and 
(f) Community Consultation and Complaints Handling.  
A7 Site Induction  
All staff employed on the site by the construction contractor 
will be required to undergo a site induction. 
Prior to construction. 
A8 Operation of Plant and Equipment 
The Proponent will ensure that all plant and equipment used 
on-site, is maintained and operated in proper and efficient 
manner, and in accordance with relevant Australian Standards. 
During operation. 
A9 Monitoring the State of Roadways 
The Proponent will monitor the state of roadways leading to 
and from the Site, during construction, and will take all 
necessary steps to clean up any adversely impacted road 
pavements as directed by the Lane Cove Council. 
During construction. 
A10 Waste Receipts  
The Proponent will ensure that a permanent record of receipts, 
for the removal of both liquid and solid waste from the Site, be 
kept and maintained up to date at all times. Such records will 
be made available to authorised person upon request. 
During construction 
and operation. 
A11 Complaints Handling 
The Proponent will prepare an Operational Complaints 
Handling Protocol for the development, prior to the 
commencement of operations.  
Prior to operation.  
A12 Soil and Water Management  
A Soil and Water Management Plan (SWMP) and Erosion and 
Sediment Control Plan (ESCP), or equivalent, will be 
implemented for the construction of the proposed 
development.  
Prior to and during 
construction. 
Specific Environmental Commitments  
Air Quality

CEMP, to be prepared for the project, as outlined in A6. 
Prior to construction.  
AQ2 The Proponent shall ensure that site-specific management and 
mitigation measures outlined in Table B10 of Appendix B of 
the AQIA are implemented.  
Prior to construction 
and at all stages of 
the project.

by SLR Consulting. 
Prior to and during 
construction.

Traffic Management Plan (CTMP).  
Prior to and during 
construction.

PLANNED MANAGEMENT AND MITIGATION MEASURES FOR SSD-67407231 
ID Management / Mitigation Measure Timing 
Cultural Heritage 
H1 An Unexpected Finds Policy will be developed, in the unlikely 
event that relics are identified during ground disturbing works.   
Prior to construction.  
H2 Unexpected Aboriginal objects remain protected by the 
National Parks and Wildlife Act 1974. If any such objects, or 
potential objects, are uncovered in the course of the activity, all 
work in the vicinity will cease immediately. A qualified 
archaeologist would be contacted to assess the find and 
Heritage NSW and Metropolitan Local Aboriginal Land Council 
would be notified. 
During construction. 
H3 If human remains, or suspected human remains, are found in 
the course of the activity, all work in the vicinity will cease, the 
site would be secured, and the NSW Police and Heritage NSW 
would be notified 
During construction. 
H4 All relevant staff, contractors and subcontractors will be made 
aware of their statutory obligations for heritage under the NSW 
Heritage Act 1977 and best practice as outlined in The Burra 
Charter 2013, during site inductions.  
Prior to construction.  
H5 The Proponent will provide a copy of the ACHA to the RAPs 
and consider all comments received. Delorean will continue to 
inform these groups about the management of Aboriginal 
cultural heritage sites within the study area throughout the life 
of the project. 
Prior to construction 
Socio-Economic 
SE1 The Proponent will notify surrounding businesses and 
residents one (1) week before commencement of construction 
activities. Notices should include: 
▪ Details of the proposal, including contact details of 
management team 
▪ Hours and expected period of construction 
▪ Details regarding process should businesses or 
residents have concerns, questions or complaints 
Prior to construction. 
SE2 The Proponent will set up a feedback process to manage and 
respond to stakeholder concerns, questions, or complaints. The 
Proponent will ensure that this process is clear and accessible 
to stakeholders such as surrounding businesses and residents. 
Prior to and during 
construction. 
SE3 The Proponent will implement the six (6) recommendations of 
the Social Impact Assessment prepared by Willowtree 
Communications. 
All stages of the 
project.  
Arboricultural 
ARB1 The following conditions are required before any work proceeds 
on site: 
• Site induction: All workers related to the construction 
process and before entering the site must be briefed about 
the requirements/conditions outlined in this report relative 
to the zone of protection, measures, and specifications 
Prior to construction.

Flooding

(FERP) for the Proposal to address Site-specific flood safety and 
operational procedures. 
Prior to operation.  
Waste Management 
WM1 The Proponent will implement a project-specific Sustainable 
Procurement Policy prior to construction works commencing.  
Prior to construction. 
WM2 Effective management of construction materials and 
construction and demolition waste, including options for reuse 
and recycling where applicable and practicable, would be 
conducted. Only wastes that cannot be cost effectively reused 
or recycled will be sent to landfill or appropriate disposal 
facilities. 
During construction.  
WM3 Waste materials produced from site preparation and 
construction activities will be separated at the source and 
stored separately on-site. 
During construction.  
WM4 The Site Manager or equivalent role will:  
▪ Arrange for suitable waste collection contractors to remove 
any construction waste from site  
▪ Ensure waste bins are not filled beyond recommended 
filling levels  
▪ Ensure that all bins and loads of waste materials leaving 
site are covered  
▪ Maintain waste disposal documentation detailing, at a 
minimum:  
o Descriptions and estimated amounts of all waste 
materials removed from site  
o Details of the waste and recycling collection 
contractors and facilities receiving the waste and  
o recyclables  
o Records of waste and recycling collection vehicle 
movements, for example, date and time of loads  
o removed, licence plate of collection vehicles, tip 
dockets from receiving facility, and  
During construction.
